Could also use rule 1 (above/below) not relevant. So maybe no rule applies. But we can note that left vs right are opposite, which is contradictory.

Thus answer: evidence 9, testimony 8.

**Step 1:** The crime photo (Evidence 2) describes the umbrella as “lying open in a distance,” indicating it is far from the victim’s body. Phoenix’s testimony (Testimony 3) says the victim “fell down on top of his cheap umbrella,” which places the umbrella directly beneath the victim (the same location).

**Step 2:** The spatial relations involved are “far from” (from the photo) versus “on top of/adjacent to” (from the testimony).

**Step 3:** Rule 15 states that if X is far from Y then Y is far from X. Rule 7 states that if X is adjacent to Y then Y is adjacent to X. The two pieces of information assign mutually exclusive relations (far vs adjacent) to the same pair (victim and umbrella), revealing a contradiction.

**Step 4:** Even without the explicit rules, the contradiction is clear: an object cannot simultaneously be at a distance and be the surface on which another object rests.

Step 1: The testimony (Testimony 0) states that Simeon was “riding the balloon to the rooftop.” Evidence 32 describes the Lion Hot Air Balloon as “used for promotional purposes. Saint is in charge of it and **drives it around on the back of a truck**.” A balloon that is attached to a truck remains at ground level; it cannot be taken up to a rooftop.

Step 2: The spatial rules that apply are:
- Rule 10: If X is below Y then Y is above X.  
- Rule 12 (same‑level) can be used to infer that the truck and the balloon share the same level (ground).

Step 3: From Evidence 32 we infer: Balloon → on → Truck (same level). The truck is on the ground, which is **below** the rooftop. By Rule 10, the rooftop is above the truck (and thus above the balloon). Therefore the balloon is **below** the rooftop. The testimony claims the balloon is **above** (riding to) the rooftop, directly contradicting the spatial relationship derived from the evidence.

Step 4: No other evidence or testimony presents a more direct conflict; this is the only pair where the described locations are mutually exclusive.

- Rule 11: If X is behind Y then Y is in front of X.
- Rule 12: If X is in front of Y then Y is behind X.

Applying Rule 12 to the evidence: “shooter is in front of victim” ⇒ “victim is behind shooter.”  
These derived statements are mutually exclusive (a victim cannot be both in front of and behind the shooter), confirming the contradiction.
